### 250

Regular Sales Data Guidelines

### 251

General requirements:

### 252

Use a CSV file with a header row; each row represents one line item.

### 253

The file name starts with sales_items and end with .csv (For example, sales_items_202505.csv).

### 254

Use UTF-8 encoding and avoid newline characters within fields.

### 255

To guarantee processing of all data in each upload, end the file with an empty line.

### 256

Keep the column order exactly as defined during onboarding—incorrect ordering causes upload failure.

### 257

Use lowercase for standard field names (For example, price, not Price).

### 258

Required fields:

### 259

Standard: item, price, order, timestamp, customer, email, and quantity.

### 260

For retail clients: Also include s_sales_channel and s_store_id.

### 261

Use customer ID (external contact ID – same as in the SAP Emarsys contact database) and, if necessary, email, but be consistent and don't mix both.​

### 262

Upload frequency and format:

### 263

The initial file covers at least two years of sales history.

### 264

Upload daily updates after initial load (Smart Insight processes data once daily).

### 265

Uploads are incremental—they add to existing data and don't overwrite it.

### 266

Avoid duplicate orders—SAP Emarsys doesn't remove duplicates.

### 267

Import sales data into Smart Insight daily to align with the Smart Insight load frequency. SAP Emarsys passes data into Smart Insight once per day.

### 268

File validation rules:

### 269

Use no more than 255 columns; keep each field under 64 KB in length.

### 270

Acceptable delimiters for SFTP upload: comma (,), semicolon (;), tab (\t), or pipe (|).

### 271

Use a standard-compliant CSV export tool/library.

### 272

Maximum file sizes:

### 273

Daily: 10 GB (uncompressed)

### 274

Hourly: 1 GB (uncompressed)

### 275

Custom fields:

_重复来源：Imp08_原文.docx；new03+04-data management_原文.docx_

### 276

Permitted data types and their header prefixes:

### 277

i_ (Integer), f_ (Float), t_ (Timestamp), s_ (String).

### 278

For example, s_book_author, i_customer_age.

### 279

Add new custom fields only at the end of the existing column list.

### 280

Don't insert or reorder existing columns—this risks data loss.​

### 281

For numeric fields, avoid empty strings, such as double quotes with no values in them (" "); use double commas (,,) for null values.

### 282

Standard Sales Data Requirements

### 283

Different sales data requirements apply to using the new Sales Data Loads solution.

### 284

The Sales Data Loads solution doesn't support Loyalty and Predict recommendation use cases.

### 285

File structure and format:

### 286

Use a .csv table with one row per line item.

### 287

Encode the file in UTF-8.

### 288

Ensure there is a header row and that the column order of the data matches this configuration.

### 289

Customize field names, but align them with the configuration.

### 290

Follow the configured naming pattern for the file name.

### 291

Max file size: 100 GB

### 292

Use any standard delimiter: comma (,), semicolon (;), tab (\t), space ( ), or pipe (|).

### 293

Properly quote newline characters when they appear; allow single and double quotes.

### 294

End the file with a full order image—include all items in the same file.

### 295

Don't upload deltas (partial updates); upload the complete transaction.

### 296

Order upload rules:

### 297

Do not update orders; upload each order only once.

### 298

Avoid re-using the same Transaction ID + Transaction Type combination more than once.

### 299

Duplicate handling (within 30 days):

### 300

If a duplicate happens within 30 days, SAP Emarsys drops the new transaction.

### 301

If a duplicate happens after 30 days, SAP Emarsys drops the old transaction.

### 302

Timestamp requirements:

### 303

Use ISO 8601 format with:

### 304

Second precision

### 305

Up to 6-digit fractional seconds

### 306

Timezone info (UTC or offset)

### 307

For example, 2022-06-16T12:18:51Z

### 308

amount_in_account_currency

### 309

amount_in_transaction_currency

### 310

Optional fields:

### 311

storeId

### 312

salesChannel

### 313

SAP Emarsys allows up to 20 custom fields.

### 314

While optional, ensure that the custom fields adhere to the correct data types.

### 315

Supported data types:

### 316

String

### 317

Float

### 318

Integer

### 319

Upload and validate sales data for the first time (through Predict):

### 320

To upload the properly formatted sales data file, go to Management > Predict Data Sources.

### 321

Access the Sales Data page by clicking ‘Set up sales data uploads.’​

### 322

To avoid duplicate data, use the same file name format with repeated uploads. Change the timestamp, for example: sales_items_20250101.CSV.

### 323

Changing the file name or uploading different files with the same orders results in duplicated data.

### 324

Use manual upload for the initial upload of historical sales data during onboarding. It allows immediate validation and error correction.

### 325

Click 'Validate/upload' file on the sales data page.

### 326

Browse and select the sales data file.

### 327

If warnings or errors appear, fix them and retry the upload.

### 328

Click 'Activate upload' to complete the upload when there are no warnings.

### 329

Remember that this process only applies to the current Predict API solution. SAP Emarsys is working on a new solution to replace it in the future.

### 330

Set up regular sales data updates through Predict:

### 331

After the initial upload of historical sales data, set up regular updates.

### 332

Send historical data in one upload, then schedule daily uploads for new purchases.

### 333

Regularly update sales data to keep Smart Insight-based reports correct.

### 334

Automate sales data imports using the current sales data API solution through Predict.

### 335

Access the Sales Data page through Management > Predict Data Sources and click 'Manage sales data.'

### 336

Click 'Show API upload details' to get setup information.

### 337

Sending sales data to Smart Insight through the Predict API influences the product recommendations and ensures real-time validation of file headers.

### 338

Duplicate data:

### 339

If a file with the same content uploads through the API, the duplicate skipped icon shows next to the 'Timestamp' in the upload history.

### 340

Split the file content into two files and upload them again.

### 341

Duplicate one of the lines in the file and upload it again.

### 342

Smart Insight customers upload sales data to SFTP servers. This legacy option has drawbacks. Consider comparing it to the current Predict sales data API. Contact an SAP Emarsys expert for further consultation to improve the process.

### 343

Limit daily sales data uploads to fewer than 10 for safe processing. Contact an SAP Emarsys expert for over 50 uploads per day.

### 344

Sales data uploads are incremental, adding new data without overwriting existing data.

### 345

Verification of the Smart Insight Data Quality

### 346

Check the status and monitor the quality of the Smart Insight data on the Smart Insight Data Quality page.

### 347

The Overview tab shows reports of Contact, Product, and Purchase data uploaded to Smart Insight and their quality.

### 348

The Data Loads tab gives information on the status of the scheduled automatic loads and recent contact, product, and purchase file uploads from the last 30 days, highlighting successful and unsuccessful tries.

### 349

Contact data field management: This section explains the importance of defining and managing system and custom contact fields before the data import. It also highlights the role of proper field mapping in supporting segmentation, personalization, and platform functionality.

### 350

System and special field usage: It introduces essential system fields such as opt-in status and language tags, along with special fields, such as admin and channel-specific fields. These fields support compliance, targeting, and customized campaigns across SAP Emarsys channels.

### 351

Contact data upload methods: Find various methods for importing and updating contact data, including manual CSV import, auto-import through SFTP/WebDAV, and real-time API integration. It also outlines the steps for guaranteeing data accuracy and deduplication.

### 352

Product and sales data integration: Upload product and sales data using CSV files or feeds, offering file format guidelines. It emphasizes the need for consistent identifiers and field structures to avoid upload errors and data mismatches.

### 353

Data validation and quality control: Verify the field mapping, remove duplicates, and check upload results. Check how to monitor the Smart Insight Data Quality page, and update schedules for ongoing data quality assurance.

Applying External Events

### 4

External Events Overview

### 5

External Events trigger automation and campaigns based on actions outside the SAP Emarsys platform. These events link to external triggers, add contacts to programs, and start emails. This setup allows smooth integration between external systems and SAP Emarsys automation flows.

### 6

On the External Events page, you can:

### 7

Create new External Events.

### 8

Delete individual or several External Events.

### 9

Search for existing events by entering their ID or name into the ‘Search’ field.​

To delete several events:

### 11

Select the events using the checkbox.

### 12

Click ‘Delete Selected.’

### 13

Review any blockers, clicking the ( - ) icon to expand details.

### 14

Resolve blockers by editing the related programs and emails. After resolving blockers, click ‘Delete External Events.’

The system automatically updates the blocker dialog as users resolve issues.

Hello, in this video, you'll learn what order, event, and payload data are. Let's say a customer places an order on your site. You can automatically trigger an order confirmation email with key details such as the customer's name, order number, and the items they have purchased. But what exactly are event data and payload? You collect the order data from the confirmation page. This is your event data. Then you format it as a JSON payload and send it to Sap, a Emarsys. That's your payload. Let's take a closer look at the event data. First, gather the customer's information name, email address, shipping details.

These product items are added as an array, allowing you to include multiple products in the confirmation email. To display this information, the email must contain placeholders that match the data fields in the payload. This is how the data is transferred to Sap, a Emarsys, enabling you to trigger emails or interactions programs, thanks for watching.

To integrate contacts into an automated program through an External Event, create a behavior-focused journey (Interactions) with the 'External Event’ program trigger. Include external data, such as purchase details, in the External Event API call. SAP Emarsys stores this data while the contact is in the program. This information helps decision-making by using attributes from this data for segmentation and content tailoring.

Transactional (triggered) messages ignore the opt-in value.

Order confirmations

Triggering a customer satisfaction survey

These events and corresponding emails happen immediately after a customer action.

### 3

Connecting Relational Data

### 4

Relational Data Overview

### 5

The SAP Emarsys Relational Data Service (RDS) integrates client-hosted databases with business-specific data into the SAP Emarsys platform. This enables segmentation and message personalization without duplicating data, making complex data accessible for these purposes.

### 6

Welcome In this short video, we explain what relational data is and how it allows you to realize the value of your data stored outside the Emarsys platform. To walk you through the relational data features, we have created a simple use case. Diego, Aisha, and Khalid purchased the product in the category football more than 90 days ago. I want to send them and other contacts like them and offer for a brand new product in the same category so that they purchase again.

### 7

发言人 00:34

### 8

First, let's go over the basics. Databases stored data in tables. If these tables can be connected, we call them relational databases. For example, you have past sales data in one of your tables here, you store the order ID, the customer email address, the product ID, price, and the date of purchase. In another table, you have your product with product I title and so on. In this case, the product ID is your relation. In our use case, the relationship between the two tables will be the football category product with the product ID. Based on this relationship, you can create views including information from both tables. Views create subsets of your actual data that provide actionable insights for you to use in your marketing strategy.

### 9

In this case, you have a view that letsgo segment and target contacts with a campaign to make people like Khalid purchase in the football category because we know he loves football, so we want to target him with related products.

### 10

发言人 01:40

### 11

This is a very simple example, but imagine it on a bigger scale. Think of the millions of customer visits and hundreds of thousands of purchases that happen every day. All of this collected data can be used to improve personalization and segmentation immeasurably. Let me show you how this works in the platform.

### 12

发言人 02:00

### 13

First, navigate to add Ons relational data. Please note that the tabs here depend on your account configuration and contract With Emarsys. On the Connections tab, I can edit and delete. Already Setup Connection. To create a new connection, I click here and choose a connection type.

### 14

发言人 02:25

### 15

Then I configure it on the Tables and Views tab. I click on the Personalization settings icon and set category status and email as reference fields. Reference fields allow you to match your contact records from external databases with the amasis contact database. With this being said, I can already find Khalid's email and his favorite category, football, in the personalization preview. Now I navigate to segment templates. By using these templates, you can use SQL to predefined complex scenarios. You can use these templates to create relational segments. Let me quickly show you how to create a new relational segment template. I select my connection, define my template, and create parameters on the left.

### 16

发言人 03:28

### 17

Then I write the SQL query on the right using my parameters. After that, I choose the reference fields and test my query. Looks good, I save my segment template, navigate to contacts segments, and create a relational segment. I name it, choose my template, and simply set the values. After saving, it is ready to use as a recipient source in my block based email.

Segmentation and Personalization in Relational Data

### 27

Link data tables from an external database to SAP Emarsys. Use the flexible Segment Template builder to create rules and select data points to be queried in the segment builder. This approach adheres to strict data privacy rules by accessing data only at execution rather than storing it.

Timezones, tables, and views:

Specify timestamps and dates/times with the correct timezone so SAP Emarsys doesn't default to CET (Europe/Vienna). Avoid setting the timezone as a reference parameter.

### 47

Security Connections- TLS/SSL:

### 48

SAP Emarsys supports server-side Transport Layer Security (TLS), the successor of the Secure Sockets Layer (SSL) protocol. SAP Emarsys uses TLS for safe server connections and encrypted communication. It verifies server certificates with root certificates for authenticity and checks certificates against the CA to prevent domain theft and attacks.

### 49

Configure server certificates in Connection Details under Relational Data Connections. Offer the root CA certificate. If signed by an intermediate CA, include the entire CA chain starting with the root CA certificate. Generally, a single root CA certificate suffices.

### 50

Connection types requiring CA certificate:

### 51

Microsoft SQL Server

### 55

### 56

SAP HANA on-premise: Configure SSL for SAP HANA (CA)

### 57

CA certificates format:

### 58

The system accepts CA certificates in Privacy-Enhanced Mail (PEM) format. Databases managed by cloud providers such as SAP HANA Cloud, Azure, Redshift, or Snowflake don't need a CA certificate.

### 80

Relational Segments in SAP Emarsys

### 81

After setting up the database connection, create Relational Segment Templates and then Relational Segments using the Relational Data. Segment Templates allow users to predefine complex segmentation scenarios with SQL queries and express them in simple sentences.

### 82

Relational Segments examples:

### 83

These examples show the power and flexibility of Relational Segments in crafting customized, effective marketing strategies.

### 84（表格/行）

Customer Purchase History | Segment customers who purchased specific products within the last six months using Relational Data. This helps tailor promotional campaigns to target repeat buyers.

### 85（表格/行）

Product Interest | Segment contacts based on their interaction with categories or products on the website, allowing for targeted follow-up campaigns promoting related products.

### 86（表格/行）

Behavior-Based Segmentation | Create segments for contacts who always engage with particular types of content, such as email newsletters or blog posts. Use this data to personalize outreach efforts that align with their interests.

### 87

Create Relational Segment Templates:

### 88

Segment Templates simplify complex segmentation scenarios by defining them through SQL queries. This simplification makes building segments easier.

### 89

Let's check how to create a segment template targeting contacts who qualify for a replenishment campaign.

### 90

Steps to create a Segment Template:

### 91

Name the segment.

### 92

Select a database using the drop-down menu.

### 93

Write the segment definition using defined parameters.

### 94

Create parameters and define variables using text, numeric, date inputs, or custom dropdowns. Then, reuse the parameters by dragging them into the SQL query.

### 95

Text input: Create a free text field that accepts alphanumeric and special characters.

### 96

Numeric input: Create a numeric field that only accepts integers, including negative values.

### 97

Date input: Create a date field in the format dd-mmm-yyyy, with an extra calendar function.

### 98

Custom dropdown: Create a single-choice list. The name appears in the segment, and the SQL query uses the list item values. Choose different option types and define filter operators for the dropdown.

### 99

Write the SQL query that underlies the segment description and extract data from the tables. Test the query first, then integrate it into the template.

### 100

Test the query, check the SQL syntax, and run the query. Adjust if necessary, based on database engine results.

### 101

Map the Relational Data reference field to the unique external contact ID (for example, externalID) for contacts in SAP Emarsys.

### 102

Preview and test. Examine the segment from a user’s perspective; test it with real data.

### 103

Run and confirm it. When seeing the correct results, click ‘Save’ to complete, confirm the query.

### 104

Avoid using decimal points in Numeric input fields. Switch to Text input fields to include decimal points.

### 105

Begin parameter names with a letter character, use only alphanumeric characters, and replace spaces with underlines (_).

### 106

Note that the timestamp and date/time show in a different timezone, yet this difference doesn't affect data storage or usage for segment calculations in SAP Emarsys.

### 107

Limit the ‘Preview’ and test function, which shows up to 10 results. Use several conditions to refine the results.

### 108

Does a correct SQL query result in a syntax error? Rerun the ‘Preview’ and test to recheck the code. Cached data triggers a false positive error message.

Relational database:

SAP Emarsys's Relational Data interprets data sets during execution, locates precise data from the Relational Data database, and applies it correctly in campaigns.

Configure tables or views for personalization by naming reference fields and adjusting the sort order of data.

Reference fields:

### 120

A reference field functions as a key, matching data between the contact database and the Relational Database. This common key helps the personalization service identify the correct data.

### 121

Data views:

If a table lacks the necessary data point, create a view that merges tables, establishing a usable data view with a correlating, referenceable data point.

Considerations for Reference and Sorting Fields:

Use at least one reference field to create a personalization preset with a specific data source.

### 125

Use the reference field as the key criterion for the personalization service to identify the first entry meeting criteria.

### 126

Use a single or various reference fields, depending on needs, to guarantee the proper order of results.

Personalization Queries on the Database

### 128

SAP Emarsys connects to databases to generate and run queries, retrieving personalized content for email campaigns using Relational Data.

### 129

SAP Emarsys opens database connections:

It connects to the database using Relational Data Service (RDS) in the following scenarios:

### 131

Using Relational Data features, such as launching email campaigns with personalization. Also, using Relational Data segments in an Automation program.

### 132

Browsing the User Interface (UI) of the SAP Emarsys to gather metadata.

### 133

Importing data through the Relational Data.

### 134

Connection pools:

### 135

Relational Data uses separate connection pools for segmentation, personalization, and data import. This design prevents interference. It also means slow segment processing doesn't hinder data import.

### 136

Connection timeout:

### 137

All connectors have a 5-second connection timeout.

### 138

Exception: Amazon Redshift has a 15-second timeout.

### 139

Generate and run personalization queries:

### 140

After establishing a database connection, SAP Emarsys generates and runs queries to retrieve personalized content.

### 141

Relational Data overview: Integrates client-hosted databases into SAP Emarsys to support segmentation and personalization using external business data without duplication.

### 142

Segmentation and personalization: Relational Data uses external data directly during campaign execution, creating complex segments and customizing messages based on defined templates.

### 143

Database connection setup: This process connects databases using supported connectors, TLS certificates, and specified credentials. It verifies the connection using a test and saves the steps.

### 144

Segment templates and examples: It builds segment templates with SQL queries and custom parameters to define dynamic marketing segments, such as purchase history or behavior.

### 145

Reference fields and personalization: This section defines reference fields to align external data with SAP Emarsys contacts and generate customized queries during campaign execution.

### 5

Connect the Shopify store to SAP Emarsys to efficiently synchronize customers, events, products and orders. Use this data within SAP Emarsys to create contact segments and personalized messaging. Build event-triggered programs and automate retention marketing strategies effectively.

### 6

### 39

Avoid legacy order data load after activation:

### 40

Triggering an initial data load after activation strains the system, extending response times. Avoid importing large volumes of historical orders, as it only updates contacts and triggers events, tiring server resources without benefits.

### 41

Steps to correct accidental triggering of data loads:

### 42

Deactivate the general data upload.

### 43

Wait for the completion of the customer data load to SAP Emarsys, which is different in duration based on the database size.

### 44

Once completed, continue uploading Shopify events, products and orders, then install web behavior tracking scripts.

Reactivate the data upload.

### 46

Request limitations:

### 47

The system limits data requests to 1000 per minute per customer.

### 48

Data load triggers:

### 49

Adjusting mappings prompts the system to re-upload all customer data to SAP Emarsys, overwriting current contact data, including opt-in information, with Shopify store values.

### 50

Field matching changes start a complete customer data upload. Consider the database size, as uploads take considerable time, pausing other updates such as subscriptions.

### 51

Plan for busy periods, such as Black Friday, to avoid starting large uploads during high traffic times. By activating data uploads:

### 52

An initial load starts for the contact data.​

### 53

An initial load doesn't start for event data.

### 54

Uploads start for order data, including the missing orders.​

### 55

Following this protocol will prevent system slowdowns and save resources.

### 113

Use the plug-in to connect Adobe Commerce, formerly known as Magento 2, with SAP Emarsys. It automatically syncs customers, events, and order data. The Adobe Commerce integration enables SAP Emarsys users to create contact segments, customize messages, and develop event-based programs to maximize and simplify marketing communication.

### 114

Import and regularly update customer data from Adobe Commerce to SAP Emarsys.

### 120

If SAP Emarsys can't update opt-in data, the following happens:

### 121

The change request enters a queue after a few retries.

### 122

The request stays in the queue for 60 minutes.

### 123

The update starts again after 60 minutes.

### 124

The plug-in repeats the process every 60 minutes for 24 hours from the point that the change request enters the queue.

### 125

If unable to update within 24 hours, the plug-in discards the opt-in change.

See an Invalid API Token error during initial connection? Check firewall settings.

### 151

Data Integration from Adobe Commerce to SAP Emarsys

### 152

Customer data:

### 153

The Adobe Commerce plug-in imports contact (whether a customer or newsletter subscriber) data into SAP Emarsys, overwriting existing values. The initial synchronization merges these contacts and potentially reduces the total number of unique contacts.

### 154

SAP Emarsys uses the email field as the external unique contact identifier for plug-ins to match contacts.

### 155

Starting with version 1.13.0 of the Adobe Commerce 2 (v2) plug-in, mapping choice fields from Adobe Commerce to SAP Emarsys fields changes synchronization to use text values instead of numeric IDs, unlike previous versions, which used numeric IDs.

### 156

Update the recommended attribute mappings, except for the non-editable fixed ID, store ID, and Accepts Marketing attributes. Adjust mappings by adding or removing attributes, but avoid unsupported multi-choice fields.

### 157

Export the initial SAP Emarsys contact database to Adobe Commerce. It helps protect contact details. Contact SAP Emarsys support to protect critical data, including opt-in status.

### 158

Finish customer uploads to SAP Emarsys before adding Adobe Commerce events or products. The time this process takes depends on the database size.

### 159

Changing field mappings triggers a full data upload, replacing existing contact data, pausing other updates, including subscribe/ unsubscribe activities.

### 160

Events:

### 161

Adobe Commerce events, such as customer registration, are available on the SAP Emarsys platform for transactional marketing, personalization, and Interaction programs.

### 162

Event handling for version 1.10.0 and later:

### 163

Enable the relevant toggle triggers for events in SAP Emarsys only. To prevent duplicates, turn off transactional emails in Adobe Commerce and consider migrating communications to SAP Emarsys.

### 164

Activate 'Trigger events also on Adobe Commerce' to make Adobe Commerce events available on both SAP Emarsys and Adobe Commerce platforms. This setup is suitable for testing and message transition.

### 165

Event handling for version 1.9.2 and earlier:

### 166

Enable events in the Events section for availability in SAP Emarsys.

### 167

Enabling event triggering automatically disables sending transactional emails from Adobe Commerce, preventing duplicate messaging. Consider migrating the relevant communications, such as order confirmation emails, from Adobe Commerce to SAP Emarsys before enabling event triggering.

### 168

Products:

### 169

Integrate product data from Adobe Commerce into SAP Emarsys using a synchronization setup.

### 170

Parent product availability depends on child products:

### 171

Parent is available if at least one child product is available.

### 172

Parent is unavailable if all child products are unavailable.

### 173

Suppose a client has a single Adobe Commerce instance with several websites connected to different SAP Emarsys accounts. In that case, the Adobe Commerce 2 (v2) plug-in’s product sync feature includes all products from these websites.

### 174

For this feature, use Adobe Commerce 2 (v2) plug-in version 1.14.5 or later.

### 175

Match custom product fields only, as standard SAP Emarsys fields automatically match. Changing the automatic matching for the standard fields could cause issues with the product catalog synchronization.

### 176

Setup and synchronization:

### 177

To set up synchronization of product data from Adobe Commerce with SAP Emarsys:

### 178

Implement Predict in SAP Emarsys before enabling product data upload.

### 179

Enable regular product data uploads in the products section.

### 180

Check the Adobe Commerce Integration/Health Check tab if the regular upload stays turned off.

### 181

Outcome:

### 182

Configured product data synchronization allows targeting customer segments based on products purchased, viewed, or left in carts, and offering tailored product recommendations.

### 183

Web Behavior tracking:

### 184

Web Extend scripts track visitor interactions on the website. The plug-in automatically installs them to process validated data for SAP Emarsys applications such as Smart Insight, Predict, and Interactions.

### 185

Orders:

### 186

Select 'Enable' in the Orders section to activate regular order uploads to SAP Emarsys.

### 187

Implement Smart Insight for proper order upload functionality. Without implementing Smart Insight, sales data is inaccessible.

### 188

Smart Insight considerations (For Adobe Commerce plug-in implemented after March 2021):

### 189

To use sales data in Smart Insight through the Adobe Commerce plug-in, select the 'Upload via Plugin' option when configuring sales data.

### 190

Starting March 10, 2021, SAP Emarsys syncs order data, including all details such as price without tax, and Smart Insight uses gross values for calculations.

### 191

Smart Insight pre-maps the needed sales data-related fields and allows attribute selection for segmentation and filtering. Use the email as a unique identifier for Adobe Commerce used through Smart Insight.

### 192

If sales data is already uploaded to SAP Emarsys, don't enable this option. In this case, contact SAP Emarsys support first so that we can prevent order duplication.

### 193

After activating the Adobe Commerce (v2) plug-in, wait about one to three days before enabling order sync.

### 194

First-time order sync pulls the entire order history to SAP Emarsys.

### 195

Adobe Commerce syncs orders to SAP Emarsys and Smart Insight only if their state is complete in Adobe Commerce, and ignores any other states.

### 196

Health Check Tab, Status and History

### 197

Health Check tab:

### 198

Access the Health Check tab to view the data synchronization status between Adobe Commerce and SAP Emarsys.

### 199

Status widget:

### 200

View the overall synchronization status, including Contacts, Orders, Products, Events, and the last update dates. Possible statuses include:

### 201

OK: The system properly syncs the data.

### 202

Data sync error: The system fails to sync data since the date shown, indicating issues retrieving contacts, order updates, or events.

### 203

X is disabled: Disable synchronization for a specific data type.

### 204

History widget:

### 205

Explore warning and error messages related to data synchronization. It shows in rows organized by type and severity level. By default, the system shows the last ten rows.

### 206

Messages include:

### 207

The system records the date and time when it receives the message.

### 208

The data type of the warning or error includes:

### 209

Contact sync

### 210

Disconnect

### 211

Order sync

### 212

Product sync

### 213

Description of the warning or error.

### 214

Use the ‘Search’ field to find specific messages.

### 215

Shopify integration overview: Connect Shopify with SAP Emarsys to sync customer, event, product, and order data for segmentation, personalization, and automation.

### 216

Shopify data sync and configuration: Sync customer data first, then upload events, products, and orders. Map fields to SAP Emarsys using email as an identifier, and sync opt-in and Smart Insight data.

### 217

Adobe Commerce (Magento) configuration: Set up plug-ins to import customer data, map fields, sync products and orders, and automate event triggers for transactional messages.

### 218

Adobe Commerce data setup: Inject Web Extend scripts through the plug-in to track customer behavior and sync product, events, orders, and web behavior tracking data for Smart Insight, Predict, personalization, and automation features.

### 219

Health and error monitoring: Use the Health Check tab, Status and History widget to track data sync between Adobe Commerce and SAP Emarsys, warning messages, and update statuses by data type.

### 3

Exploring SAP CX Integrations and Connectors

### 4

SAP CX Solutions and SAP Engagement Cloud - Emarsys Edition Integration Overview

### 5

Integrating other SAP CX solutions with SAP Emarsys improves functionality by mapping data, making it useful across the various SAP solutions. Commerce Cloud (CC) acts as a shop, such as Shopify or Adobe Commerce (Magento). The Customer Data Cloud (CDC) manages customer profiles. The Customer Data Platform (CDP) collects engagement data for a wider view. The SAP Emarsys Account Engagement add-on improves the SAP Emarsys platform's B2B functionality, integrating with SAP Sales Cloud and SAP Commerce Cloud to support comprehensive processes.

### 6

Let’s discover how to connect and set up these systems to support smooth operations with SAP Emarsys.

### 7

Commerce Cloud Integration

### 8

SAP Commerce Cloud integration (CC) with SAP Emarsys enables smooth data exchange between systems. This integration automatically syncs customers, events, and products. The integration offers real-time customer and event synchronization.

### 9

### 10

Simple system connection.

### 11

Customer synchronization.

### 12

Optional consent synchronization.

### 13

Real-time event handling.

### 14

Native support for various Tactics.

### 15

Product synchronization.

### 16

### 17

Integration with the latest version of SAP Commerce Cloud guarantees smooth operation. Older, unsupported releases present compatibility issues.

### 100

Customer Data Cloud Integration

### 101

This integration brings data from SAP Customer Data Cloud (CDC) into SAP Emarsys. It allows importing of merged contact data. This enables marketers to run campaigns based on master data.

### 102

Master data updates in SAP CDC trigger automatic transfers to SAP Emarsys. This makes it possible for near-real-time processing. The integration manages up to 1000 requests per minute, but high data volumes can lead to temporary slowdowns in SAP Emarsys.

### 103

Restrictions:

### 104

Integration restrictions apply to the following items:

### 105

Real-time:

### 106

Data updates trigger in real-time, but traffic density can affect speed. Synchronization usually happens in seconds but can sometimes take longer.

### 107

SAP Customer Data Cloud allows selection of 50 custom fields maximum.

### 108

SAP Emarsys doesn't support connections to both SAP Customer Data Cloud and SAP Customer Data Platform. First, integrate SAP CDC with SAP CDP. Then, connect SAP CDP to SAP Emarsys to access data from the SAP Customer Data Cloud.

Set up both systems and refer to the Help Portal documentation for more onboarding details.

Activate customer profile synchronization.

### 116

Skipping ‘Try to Match by Email’ causes duplication and system issues in SAP Emarsys.

### 117

Similarly to SAP Commerce Cloud and Customer Data Platform, email matching is only a supplementary step in Customer Data Cloud integration. Email isn't the primary contact identification method. The system uses customer IDs for ongoing identification, updates, and deletions.

### 118

For SAP Emarsys accounts created after February 2025, raise a support ticket to ask a WSSE API user. WSSE is transitioning to OpenID Connect with OAuth 2.0 client credentials permission.

Custom Fields, Consent and Subscriptions

### 136

Create data fields in SAP Customer Data Cloud, and they automatically sync with SAP Emarsys. Custom fields map from SAP CDC to SAP Emarsys.

### 137

Naming convention: New fields follow naming conventions:

### 138

CD_C_* for consent

### 139

CD_S_* for subscription

### 140

CD_T_* for communication topics

### 141

CD_P_* for profile

### 142

CD_D_* for data including shared fields

### 143

CD_* historically for shared fields

### 144

Define and modify custom fields:

### 145

The field string ID is always distinct and in lowercase. The field name only accepts a change in case from upper to lower or conversely. The system doesn’t support other renaming.

### 146

Deleting custom fields:

### 147

To keep integration intact, avoid deleting CD-ID.

### 148

When disconnecting the SAP CDC and SAP Emarsys, or removing them with the schema editor, the system deletes all fields, along with their data, except those fields in use by SAP Emarsys and CD-ID.

### 149

Establish single- and multi-choice custom fields:

### 150

SAP Emarsys API creates all fields as text and transfers values as plain text, such as 0-opt-in and 1-opt-out.

### 151

Data field type:

### 152

Create data fields by selecting the data field type, which can be string, numeric, date, or boolean. Linking to systems such as SAP Emarsys enables this feature.

### 153

Creating consents:

### 154

When creating custom consents:

### 155

Compile necessary consents for marketing and select only these consents for replication.

### 156

The system shows consents in SAP Emarsys as custom fields with the prefix CD_C_*.

### 157

Choose one consent in SAP CDC for two-way email synchronization, considering that SMS consent is optional. The system adapts the consents for use, such as marketing.email and marketing.sms in SAP Emarsys.

### 158

Map other consents, such as marketing.mobile, if they don't link to email or SMS. They have one-way synchronization.

### 159

Subscriptions:

### 160

Opt-in and opt-out subscriptions from SAP Customer Data Cloud automatically sync with SAP Emarsys.

### 161

For custom subscription fields:

### 162

Names in SAP Emarsys begin with CD_S_*.

### 163

Create subscription fields during schema updates by adding them to the mapping.

### 164

Customer Data Platform Integration

### 165

The SAP Customer Data Platform (CDP) integration imports data from SAP Customer Data Platform into SAP Emarsys. It enables the import of merged contact data. This process allows the execution of campaigns based on the imported master data. Updates to master data automatically transfer from the source system to SAP Emarsys, keeping the data up-to-date and actionable.

### 166

The system triggers data updates in real-time, but traffic density can affect timing. Synchronization usually finishes in seconds or under a minute, but depending on the amount of data, it sometimes takes longer.

### 167

Limitations exist on custom fields from SAP Customer Data Platform:

### 168

Up to 40 string fields, each of them 255 characters max.

### 169

Up to 100 numeric, date, or boolean fields.

### 170

There are several necessary API permissions needed for the SAP Emarsys API user to enable SAP Customer Data Platform integration, such as contact.create. For more information, check the Help Portal page.

### 171

SAP Customer Data Platform and SAP Customer Data Cloud can't both be connected to SAP Emarsys. In a case that needs data from both, make sure data from SAP Customer Data Cloud flows to SAP Customer Data Platform first.

### 172

As with SAP Commerce Cloud, Customer Data Platform, and Customer Data Cloud integrations, the system uses email matching only during the initial creation of contacts. For later updates and deletions, customer IDs are the main identifiers, not email addresses, which helps keep proper synchronization and prevents duplicates.

### 173

Synchronized Activities Between Customer Data Platform and SAP Emarsys

### 174

Opened Email: This activity also includes all attributes from Sent Email, such as IP address, Time zone, and City name.

### 175

Clicked Email: This activity includes all Sent Email and Opened Email attributes, such as the Email click date-time and Continent code (IP-based).

### 176

Bounced Email: This activity also includes all attributes from Sent Email, such as Bounce type.

### 177

Email Complaints: This activity also includes all attributes from Sent Email, such as Complaint date and time.

### 197

Corporate Accounts for B2B Data

### 198

This enhancement lets organizations use corporate account and relationship data alongside contact data. It replicates information within the SAP Emarsys corporate account model, offering B2B data, which is useful for marketing engagement activities.

### 199

Extra data replicated between SAP CDP and SAP Emarsys includes:

### 200

Corporate accounts

### 201

Relationships

### 202

Changes to corporate accounts and relationships.

### 203

Structural changes in the data schema.

### 204

Use cases for corporate account data:

### 205

LinkedIn campaigns

### 206

Personalizations

### 207

Prerequisite:

### 208

Activate SKU 8013363: SAP Emarsys, Account Engagement option, and the basic license.

### 209

Set up master data with profiles (contacts), relationships, and accounts, and define processing purposes for each.

### 210

Limitations:

### 211

Consider these limitations for corporate accounts in B2B scenarios:

### 212

The platform doesn't currently support extensibility and custom fields, but plans to support them in the future.

### 213

After deleting a profile in SAP Customer Data Platform, the system can't remove the corresponding relationships. Instead, remove relationships before deleting the contact profile.

### 214

Account Engagement Overview

### 215

The SAP Emarsys Account Engagement (SAP Account Engagement) add-on improves the sap Emarsys platform's B2B functionality, integrating with SAP Sales Cloud and SAP Commerce Cloud to support comprehensive processes. It uses B2B data from these systems, offering the following options:

### 216

Tactics for quick market entry.

### 217

Sales collaboration for marketing-led and sales aligned campaigns with key context from sales accounts.

### 218

Dashboards and Reporting for comparing campaign results and understanding drivers of engagement and sales.

### 219

This section shows information relevant to setting up SAP Account Engagement with SAP Sales Cloud version 1. It highlights features specific for version 2 or 1. Otherwise, they apply to both versions 1 and 2.

### 220

Limitations for the setup with SAP Sales Cloud version 2:

### 221

The setup of SAP Account Engagement with SAP Sales Cloud Version 2 imposes the following restrictions:

### 222

The system excludes replicating or importing extension fields and code lists created in SAP Sales Cloud.

### 223

The system doesn't support the replication or import of marketing permissions.

### 224

The system doesn't support lead engagement processes, including proposed activities and lead Webhook node presets, or Tactics using Webhook presets.

### 225

The setup ignores creating and reviewing sales campaigns in sales collaboration. Turn off permissions in SAP Emarsys to hide the Sales Campaign screen.

### 226

The system doesn't filter contact data for replication and import into SAP Emarsys.

### 227

The system doesn't support selecting a method to match imported contact data with existing contacts in SAP Emarsys.

### 228

This list shows the details available for corporate accounts. These details can support certain B2B scenarios such as 'on-behalf emails' or 'sales collaboration.'

Name

ABC Classification

Industry

Search or filter by specific columns by country/region, ABC classification, and industry through ‘Contacts’ to show import counts.

Manage the corporate account lists on the ‘Corporate Account Lists’ screen.

### 262

Use Cases for Tactics in SAP Account Engagement

### 263

SAP Account Engagement supports various use cases with Tactics:

### 264

Win new customers.

### 265

Succeed by supporting customers (the Tactics ‘Request to Be Contacted’, ‘Set Lead Qualification Stages’ and ‘Replicate New Marketing Contact’ are only available in SAP Sales Cloud Version 1).

### 266

Grow existing customer base.

### 267

Automate business processes (use case for SAP Sales Cloud version 1).

### 268

The Strategic Dashboard isn't available in SAP Account Engagement. The webhook node preset and lead engagement are only available for SAP Sales Cloud version 1.

### 269

Webhook node preset (for SAP Sales Cloud version 1):

### 270

Use Webhook node presets in Tactics to establish communication links between SAP Emarsys and SAP Sales Cloud.

### 271

These presets enable the system to send data to SAP Sales Cloud when contacts reach the webhook node in Interactions.

### 272

Two-way data transfer isn't available for Tactics related to SAP Sales Cloud Version 2.

### 273

Lead engagement (for SAP Sales Cloud version 1):

### 274

Let’s check the topics of the lead qualification process:

### 275

Collect signals: Gather the interest shown by contacts as they engage to evaluate their buying intent.

### 276

Match: Match contacts using their email addresses. If the system finds no match, create a new contact (lead) in SAP Sales Cloud.

### 277

Profile: Use contact or account data to profile contacts or accounts into clusters.

### 278

Score: Assign values to the collected signals to adjust the Contact Engagement Score.

### 279

Decide: Use scores to determine the lead's current stage for nurturing or handing over to sales.

### 280

Execute new actions: Transfer contacts to other programs or pass to SAP Sales Cloud to create leads for sales people to follow up.

### 281

Sales Collaboration

### 282

Let’s examine how marketing and sales collaborate to create customized email campaigns for sales representatives.

### 283

The marketer defines the target audience for the campaign.

### 284

Overview:

### 285

The marketer creates the campaign in SAP Account Engagement and chooses the collaboration level with sales. Options include:

### 286

Email campaign: Create and send emails for default sales reps, using their specific details for personalization, without a sales review. SAP Sales Cloud defines the default sales rep.

### 287

Email for sales campaigns (for SAP Sales Cloud version 1): Add sales rep data and request reviews. Sales reps can adjust the content and recipients using the send-on-behalf feature. Marketers adjust the email accordingly before sending it.

### 288

Preparation:

### 289

Design an email template for the campaign.

### 290

Setting up an on-behalf email campaign:

### 291

Marketers create campaigns that include details of the default sales representatives. The system sends these emails to the sales reps' contacts without review by the sales reps.

### 292

Setting up a sales campaign with review (for SAP Sales Cloud version 1):

### 293

The marketer creates a campaign for review by sales representatives.

### 294

The sales rep reviews the content and audience and offers feedback to the marketer.

### 295

The marketer tracks changes and comments resulting from the sales rep's review.

### 296

The marketer uses the ‘Collaboration’ icon in the ‘Sales Campaign’ overview to adjust or resolve problems before launching the campaign.

### 297

Account Engagement Reporting

### 298

SAP Account Engagement offers standard SAP Emarsys reports tailored to B2B business cases. Measure engagement outcomes against KPIs for emails, trends, mobile, and registrations.

### 299

Start all reports from the tailored Home Dashboard, with adaptations available, adjusting them as needed. Some SAP Emarsys configuration options are unavailable.

### 300

The available reports include:

### 301

Emails

### 302

Mobile Reporting

### 303

Registrations

### 304

Integration landscape overview: To centralize customer data, integrate SAP Emarsys with SAP Commerce Cloud, Customer Data Cloud, Customer Data Platform, and Sales Cloud.

### 305

Commerce Cloud integration: Sync contacts, events, and products with real-time updates. Enable Web Extend before product sync and manage consent handling.

### 306

Customer Data Cloud setup: Import contacts, consents, and subscriptions into SAP Emarsys using mapped custom fields and real-time sync with naming conventions.

### 307

Customer Data Platform integration: Transfer merged customer profiles and engagement data for segmentation, reporting, and personalization with field and speed constraints.

### 308

Account Engagement add-on: Improve B2B use cases by connecting with Sales and Commerce Cloud, managing corporate accounts, and enabling sales collaboration campaigns.

Contact Fields Overview

### 23

SAP Emarsys provides standard system fields for storing contact data. Depending on your business needs, you can create your own custom fields.

### 24

System Fields:

### 25

Every SAP Emarsys account contains a standard set of contact data fields such as Salutation, First Name, Last Name, Email, and so on.

### 26

These system fields can neither be edited nor deleted.

### 27

If you need a system field to be defined differently, for example, because you want to include more or other options, you must create a copy of the system field and rename it; the new field will then behave like a custom field.

### 28

Product Fields:

### 29

These fields are created depending on your product package and the add-ons you’ve purchased; you have specific field details such as the channel-specific opt-in field, Newsletter field, Interests field, and " Do not track me" field.

### 30

Product fields must not be deleted or renamed, as this will cause the respective product to stop working. Since they are enabled individually for each account, field IDs will vary from account to account.

### 31

Permitted users of the platform can create custom fields. They can be edited, deleted, or copied as required.

Check out this video to learn more about other Data Management features!

Data Import Overview

the initial upload of your contacts, during the onboarding phase

when the use of the SAP Emarsys API is unsuitable, for example, uploading lists of contacts collected offline.

Segments

On the right, you have controls to combine, refresh, edit, copy, or delete each segment. You can delete multiple segments at the same time. A warning dialog will check and show you where the selected segments are being used. At the top, you can search for segments. Click here to set more filters, such as showing only combined segments. Let's create a new segment Here you have two options, create a standard segment or a combined segment. Standard segments depend on your account configuration. Thanks for your attention.

Web Behavior Segment

Relational Segment

SMS Segment

### 9

Hello, in this video, you'll learn more about how to build segments based on contact criteria. First, you'll need to give your segment a name and optionally a description. Next, you can select to have this segment's criteria run against your entire database or only on a specific contact list. Scrolling down, you'll find the filter conditions available and they are categorized into different types, contact, email and mobile behavior, geolocation, and depending on your account setup. Smart Insight When working with contact criteria, you will be using only the contact data fields in your Emarsys database. Depending on the field type, different conditions are available. To build nested filters, you'll need to link criteria with and or or operators which you can switch on by clicking Advanced Filter Options.

For example I'm interested in contacts from Germany or France whose birthday will be in exactly 30 days. And who have opted in email marketing? Thanks for your attention.

Combined Segments

### 12

With SAP Emarsys, you can combine multiple segments and contact lists to target your contacts accurately. You can also create multi-layered, complex segments, which are much more efficient than having many side-by-side segments. Let’s check out how to create combined segments.

### 13

Enter your segment name and add a description.

### 14

Specify your target audience.

### 15

You can link elements within a subgroup using the Match all (AND) or Match any (OR) operator.

### 16

Add a segment or contact list by clicking Add Criteria.

### 17

Add another subgroup by clicking Add Subgroup.

### 18

You can exclude segments, contact lists, or groups by clicking Add Exclusion. These can be linked using the AND, AND NOT or OR operators.

### 19

Click Hide All Details in the top right corner to collapse all groups and subgroups. As a result, details of all groups and subgroups will be hidden, and only the name of the groups and the number of criteria within all groups will be displayed.

### 20

### 21

When you are ready, click Save.

### 3

Introducing Automations

### 4

SAP Emarsys Automation Programs

### 5

With SAP Emarsys you can create automated customer engagement journeys designed to create pathways. This approach uses flexible and interconnected nodes with an entry point that goes through actions and channels until reaching an endpoint. You can find four types of programs.

### 6

Please note that the available automation program types, event triggers, and nodes may differ depending on your packaging or implementation.

### 7

Automation Center VS. Interactions​

### 8

Let’s check out the difference between Automation Center and Interactions programs in SAP Emarsys!

### 9

Automation Center vs Interactions

### 10

Automation Center

### 11

Lifecycle campaigns for a large contact base.

### 12

用于面向大量联系人运行生命周期类营销活动。

### 13

Campaigns based on lifecycle data activities.

### 14

基于生命周期数据和活动来设计营销活动。

### 15

Pre-scheduled and recurring campaigns.

### 16

适合提前排期、周期性重复执行的营销活动。

### 17

Multi-level ad-hoc programs.

### 18

支持多层级的临时性或特定目的自动化项目。

### 19

Interactions

### 20

Time-critical, rule-based, automated communication at scale.

### 21

适合大规模、规则驱动、对时间敏感的自动化沟通。

### 22

Immediate reaction to customer behavior.

### 23

能够即时响应客户行为。

### 24

Building activity and event-driven journeys.

### 25

用于构建基于活动和事件触发的客户旅程。

### 26

Faster and more reliable reaction (better deliverability).

### 27

响应更快、更可靠，送达表现更好。

You can choose various event options depending on your goal. I choose the web Extend event in the editor. I name my program and save it.

Automation Center Overview​

### 44

Automation Center allows designing, validating, and automating engagement programs ranging from simple transactional mailings to sophisticated, multi-step, multi-channel programs via an intuitive user interface. Exploring Email Analytics

### 4

Email Analytics Overview

### 5

Email Analysis Page

### 6

The Campaign Reporting tab gives you an overview of all the launched campaigns. You can easily filter and search for specific campaigns. The plus sign means the email has child campaigns, like weekly running newsletter campaigns or AB versions. Click on the "show" icon to see more details.

### 7

2. The Campaign Launch Monitoring tab lets you check the launch progress for large campaigns. As the data on the page refreshes automatically, you get an up-to-date estimation.

### 8

On the Trends page, you can view different response metrics for multiple email campaigns over time, giving you an insight into longer-term changes in your contacts’ behavior. You can also select campaigns by their launch date and category, search for individual campaigns and compare them side by side.

### 9

Let’s discover these pages!

### 10

Welcome In this session, we will focus on email analytics. We introduce these pages through a post purchase upsell campaign use case. The trend reporting shows the performance of the last 30 days of this campaign series. If you prefer, you can define other custom ranges. Below are all metrics for this campaign, the number of total emails sent, revenue purchases, open and click rates, summaries on delivered emails, unsubscribe rates, bounces, and complaints. Here I can choose which metrics I want to display, and I can also export the raw data as a CSV file. The results summary shows all basic metrics for the selected launch of this campaign series, such as number of emails sent or breakdowns of the bounces.

### 11

Deliverability reporting is a powerful and important tool to identify deliverability issues on an ISP level. This report analyzes the response rates for the 10 most popular domains in your launch list and compares them with the campaign average engagement reporting shows who is interacting with your emails to help you understand if you are targeting and segmenting correctly. Let's compare it to other emails on the Trends page. While in the trend reporting, you see the performance of a specific campaign series. Here you can view different response metrics for multiple email campaigns. On the Comparison tab, I select the campaigns I'd like to compare. The Revenue Reporting tab gives you a detailed overview of your conversions. I hope that you enjoyed this session and thank you for your attention.

### 12

Exploring Best Practices

### 13

Best Practices

### 14

Data Management & User Interaction:

### 15

Keep your data clean and prevent future issues by identifying the oldest, least active, invalid, and non-existent contacts and removing them from your systems.

### 16

Emarsys automatically excludes the hard bounces to help you avoid further deliverability issues. For this, you can configure bounce management rules.

### 17

Email Engagement:

### 18

Be customer-centric and react to customers' needs. Create relevant and engaging content. For example, implement a countdown to show the last day you can still get the product delivered before Christmas.

### 19

Ask for feedback from your customers. For example, with a post-purchase feedback request program, you can identify your promoters and detractors to measure satisfaction and drive repeat purchases.

### 20

Be innovative and unique to attract and engage contacts. For example, use gamification or create your special event, like "Green Wednesday."

### 21

Email Marketing Strategy:

### 22

Send to contacts that gave their permission.

### 23

Use different content depending on the contact's lifecycle stage. For example, send email content aligned to your blog and social media content to your inactive contacts to give them a reason to open and click through to your website.

### 24

Send emails to inactive contacts less often than active ones to balance the total monthly email volume.

### 25

Make your newsletter sign-up and unsubscribe processes transparent and inform your contacts what kind of content they will receive and why.

### 26

Ask your subscribers for their preferences on the content they would like to receive and their preferred newsletter frequency.

### 27

Content Management:

### 28

Ensure the sender is easily recognized as your brand by using the subdomain of your website domain as your sender. Use separate subdomains for different email streams, such as marketing and transactional emails.

### 29

Make the subject line clear and reflect the purpose of the email; you only have 50-70 characters. But keep your subject lines close to 50 characters to make the recipient want to open it.

### 30

Avoid using sensationalist phrases that could push your mail into a recipient's spam email folder. For example, instead of "Free Delivery, Buy Now!" use "Want rock star service?".

### 31

Use personalization to increase the engagement rate, including a customer’s name or other personal data, such as location or interests.

### 32

Design the main body content for the email's purpose and make it concise. The email should create interest through clear sections, bullet points, and images and draw attention to your Calls to Action.

### 33

Make your footer customer-centric to help recipients resolve issues, unsubscribe, and change communication preferences. This is important, because the footer is where recipients go when there is a problem.

### 34

Automations:

### 35

Use combined segments instead of consecutive ones.

### 36

Make sure your programs are simple enough. Split them using the "Entry from another Program" node.

### 37

Analytics:

### 38

Use click rates, conversions, and revenue attributed to email campaigns or website visits as your main KPIs for your email marketing.

### 39

Check email reporting pages regularly to see their performance and spot issues, such as high complaints and unsubscribe rates.

### 40

Check deliverability reporting to see if you have unusually high/low metrics, such as a high bounce rate on a specific domain.

### 41

Rebuild the sender's reputation if you are experiencing bounces, blocks, or loss of inbox placement (spam folder). Temporarily send emails only to new and active contacts in separate campaigns to show new data's bounce and response rates. New data should have low bounces and very high response rates.

Please check out our SAP Emarsys Deliverability Homepage for further details.

### 3

Using AI-Powered Guidance to Accelerate SAP Customer Experience Projects

### 4

SAP Joule for Consultants Demo

### 5

Empower your project teams to deliver faster with confidence—every step of the way—with SAP Joule for Consultants, conversational AI built to help consulting teams move faster, make better decisions, and deliver high-quality outcomes.

### 6

In this video, we'll explore an Sap Joul for consultants, an AI powered assistant designed to accelerate Sap cloud transformations and empower consulting teams. Sap Cloud transformations are strategic, but also complex. What if your consultants had trusted expert level assistants at their fingertips 24 sevenths? Meet Sap Joul for Consultants, conversational AI built to help consulting teams move faster, make better decisions, and deliver high quality outcomes. With Jul consultants get real time answers grounded in Sap's most authoritative and exclusive content, like best practices, implementation steps, and design recommendations, all expertly curated and kept up to date by Sap, the most comprehensive Sap knowledge base instantly accessible to guide project teams from planning to delivery consultants using Joule complete projects up to 14% faster, helping clients realize value sooner. Sap Joul for consultants simplifies even the most complex requests from discovering Sap Cloud transformation toolchains to resolving configuration challenges with step by step guidance that reduces rework by to 50, it supports fit gap analysis to match solutions to customer requirements and generate simple Abap and CDS code from natural language prompts. It can even explain Abap code to help consultants understand 40% faster, all powered by exclusive fine tuned Sap specific llds. By eliminating time consuming knowledge searches, it saves consultants up to 1.5 hours per day, boosting their productivity and efficiency across the board, whether junior or expert consultants work smarter and more confidently with AI that scales their knowledge and accelerates their work, empower your project teams to deliver faster with confidence every step of the way.

### 7

With Sap Jewel for Consultants. This concludes our overview of Sap Joul for consultants and how AI is transforming the way consultant teams deliver Sap Cloud projects.

### 8

Joule for Consultants - Best Practices for SAP CX

### 9

You are talking to a Retrieval system First

### 10

Think of it as a AI Librarian, where the librarian is a really good (!) probability model

### 11

The results are Confidence based, highest probability answers

### 12

What to remember when using a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) system like Joule for Consultant (J4C):

### 13

This slide introduces best practices for consultants using Joule within SAP CX, focusing on how to effectively work with a RAG system like J4C. It sets the foundation by framing the system as a probability-driven "AI librarian," helping consultants understand that responses are retrieval-based and confidence-weighted rather than definitive statements of fact.

### 14

Prompting Strategies for Joule for Consultants

### 15

Strategy1: Explicitly Request Multi-Document Summarization

### 16

Examples

### 17

Summarize insights from multiple sources on how SAP CX improves customer engagement and satisfaction.

### 18

Summarize insights from multiple sources on how SAP CX enhances the customer journey across different touchpoints.

### 19

Why

### 20

Ensures system retrieves and synthesizes multiple chunks, rather than relying on just one.

### 21

Strategy2: Ask for Supporting Evidence

### 22

Provide documented case studies of companies that have successfully implemented SAP CX to enhance their customer service and support strategies.

### 23

Provide real-world examples of how SAP CX has improved customer data management and personalization in global organizations.

### 24

Encourages AI to prioritize retrieval of factual, document-backed responses rather than just generating a guess.

### 25

Strategy3: Enforce Step-by-Step Reasoning

### 26

Explain the step-by-step process for setting up SAP CX for new customer onboarding.

### 27

Encourages logical structuring of retrieved information and works well for process-driven queries (Q2C, Finance workflows, BTP integrations...).

### 28

Strategy4: Generate Actionable Recommendations

### 29

Explain step-by-step how SAP CX integrates with SAP Commerce Cloud for a seamless customer experience.

### 30

Suggest three best practices for implementing SAP CX to improve customer satisfaction and loyalty.

### 31

Suggest three ways SAP CX can optimize the customer experience in a competitive market.

### 32

Retrieves prescriptive, solution-driven insights and helps consultants make informed decisions.

### 33

Prompting strategies in Joule for consultants are practical guidelines for asking clear, specific, and context-rich questions to get more accurate and relevant responses from a RAG-based AI system. By using domain terminology, structured requests, and iterative refinement, consultants can significantly improve the quality, precision, and usefulness of Joule’s answers for SAP CX projects.

### 34

Advanced prompting strategies in Joule for Consultants help consultants extract deeper, more reliable insights by intentionally requesting comparisons, multi-document summaries, evidence, and structured reasoning. These techniques guide the system to retrieve and synthesize richer information, enabling more actionable, context-aware recommendations for SAP projects.

### 35

SAP Joule for Consultants – For SAP CX

### 36

SAP Joule for Consultants serves as a conversational AI solution that accelerates SAP CX implementations and transformations by providing real-time, expert-level guidance grounded in SAP's most comprehensive knowledge base. For CX consultants, Joule acts as an intelligent copilot that streamlines customer experience project delivery through instant access to configuration guidance, best practices, and code intelligence across the entire SAP CX portfolio.

### 37

发言人 02:40

### 38

In this video, we'll explore Joul for consultants and demonstrate how AI powered assistants can streamline implementation tasks across various Sap solutions. This demonstration focuses on examples through different personas, and we're going to start with the consultant persona. We'll examine a number of areas that consultants or architects typically seek for content, examples being enablement and discovery, guidance and instructions, troubleshooting, and perhaps more technical support. We'll then have a look at a different persona. So this isn't just restricted to consultants, although the tool is called Joul for consultants, project managers, and anyone involved in implementations will find this useful.

### 39

We have prepared example prompts to demonstrate functionality, these are illustrative examples designed to showcase the tool's you will have access to this sandbox and you can go and try your own prompts. For example, in an enablement and discovery use case. Please give me an overview of data flows between Sap Commerce Cloud and S for Hana. ### 3

Defining Omnichannel Marketing

### 4

Omnichannel Marketing Definition

### 5

Omnichannel marketing is a comprehensive approach that synchronizes various channels, including online and offline methods such as social media and physical stores, to provide customers with a consistent and tailored experience. This engages customers in personalized communications across all channels, enhancing customer interaction, boosting satisfaction and loyalty, and leading to higher sales conversions.

### 6

### 8

### 27

Multichannel Vs. Omnichannel Marketing

### 28

Multichannel and omnichannel marketing have several key differences. These depend on how each approach handles and integrates customer interactions across various channels.

### 29

Multichannel Marketing:​

### 30

Multichannel marketing involves using multiple independent channels to reach and interact with customers.​

### 31

Channels operate in silos, with little to no interaction between them. Each channel (for example, email, social media, physical store) functions independently and may have its own strategies and metrics.​

### 32

Each channel can offer a different experience, potentially causing the customer to feel disconnected. When customers switch channels, they may be required to start over or provide the same information multiple times.​

### 33

The primary focus is on the channels themselves and maximizing their effectiveness.

### 34

Omnichannel Marketing:​

### 35

Omnichannel marketing prioritizes the overall customer journey, ensuring a smooth, continuous experience across all channels. Customers can switch between channels without losing context or repeating actions.​

### 36

It integrates product, sales, and customer data across digital and physical channels to provide a seamless experience regardless of where customers interact with the brand.

### 37

All channels are interconnected, sharing data and interactions to offer a unified customer experience.​

### 38

This approach allows marketers to deliver highly personalized cross-channel engagements, ensuring the right message reaches the right customer at the right time. It focuses on consistency and personalization across all touchpoints.​

### 39

In summary, while multichannel marketing focuses on using multiple channels independently, omnichannel marketing integrates all channels to provide a cohesive and consistent customer experience.

### 40

Omnichannel

### 41

SAP Emarsys offers an omnichannel platform with various features to enhance the omnichannel approach. These include strategic and predictive tools, diverse channels, automated and integrated solutions, and capabilities for omnichannel personalization.​

### 42

SAP Emarsys' mission is to empower marketers worldwide to create personalized omnichannel experiences that deliver business outcomes.

### 43

Channels and Benefits

### 44

Here are the SAP Emarsys channels and their key highlights:​

### 45

Email: Offers personalized communication and advanced features to make content relevant and engaging. It is a cost-effective and widely accessible channel. ​

### 46

SMS: Provides quick and direct customer communication, ideal for time-sensitive updates and exclusive promotions. ​

### 47

Mobile Engage: Allows businesses to send personalized messages directly to customers' mobile devices via their own application.

### 48

Push: Delivers concise, targeted messages directly to the lock screen or notification center for immediate visibility.​

### 49

In-App: Enhances user engagement within the app. Suitable for recommendations or rewarding loyal application users.​

### 50

Inbox: Sends direct messages within the app's inbox. Practical for sending condition and term updates.

### 51

Mobile Wallet: Delivers mobile pass campaigns that can be used in email or web campaigns. These campaigns send digital vouchers and coupons directly to Apple or Google Wallet for easy in-store redemption.​

### 52

​Web Channel: Showcases personalized content or messages on your website. Drive conversions by using it at the right time, for example, when a website visitor is about to leave. ​

### 53

​Web Push: Sends notifications to engage customers who have opted in. It is designed to share updated information and promotions while browsing online.​

### 54

​Digital Ads: Connects with social media platforms for targeted advertising to expand customer reach and optimize ad spend. It is capable of targeting look-alike audiences and integrating lead ad forms to broaden your customer base.​

### 55

​Direct Mail: Adds a personal touch to your marketing strategy by sending physical promotional materials directly to customers' mailboxes.

### 56

Omnichannel Marketing Features

### 57

SAP Emarsys provides a collection of features designed to strengthen omnichannel marketing. These include:​

### 58

Automation: This feature streamlines and interconnects marketing workflows and campaigns across multiple channels for greater efficiency.​

### 59

Segmentation: This allows building audience segments utilizing data from various sources, such as email, web, and SMS behavior data.​

### 60

Personalization: Enables customizing messages and content to be aligned with individual customer preferences and interactions, maintaining consistency across all channels.​

### 61

Omnichannel Voucher Management: Facilitates sending unique codes to contacts across multiple channels.​

### 62

Emarsys Loyalty: This tool helps create personalized loyalty experiences, enables tracking of customer behavior, and offers exclusive rewards to forge deeper connections.

### 63

A/B Testing: This feature fosters experimentation with differing messaging and content variants to augment campaign performance.​

### 64

Individual Channel Dashboards and Reporting: Allows monitoring of campaign performance metrics and provides the capability of analyzing results per channel.​

### 65

Campaign Analytics: This ensures the coordination and synchronization of marketing activities across various channels.​

### 66

Audience Reporting: This tool helps users better understand target audiences by offering comparative metrics on custom groups of contacts across multiple channels.

### 67

These features jointly empower marketers to create a seamless and personalized customer journey across all touchpoints, enhancing engagement, loyalty, and conversions.

### 68

Exploring Channel Interconnections with Tactics and Automation

### 69

Automation Overview

### 70

SAP Emarsys provides Automation solutions to help you build audience-focused or contact behavior-based automated journeys. It allows you to create complex programs across multiple channels, such as customer reactivation or cart abandonment.​

### 71

SAP Emarsys also offers Tactics. These are ready-made programs you can tailor to your needs.​

### 72

The key to an omnichannel approach is Automation, which connects various channels seamlessly. ​

### 73

This lesson provides a simple example to help you understand the broad possibilities of SAP Emarsys's omnichannel capabilities.​

### 74

Use Case: Abandoned Browse

### 75

In this use case, our goal is to transform casual website visitors into loyal customers by enhancing their engagement. We use various channels and tools to guide these contacts back to your site and implement web campaigns to incentivize them to purchase.​

### 4

Loyalty Overview

### 5

Emarsys Loyalty is designed to enhance customer loyalty by personalizing user experience based on tracked behavior and purchases. It aims to boost long-term customer value and return on investment and can be managed directly by marketers without IT support.​

### 6

Let’s check out the basics of Emarsys Loyalty!

### 7

Welcome In this short video, we'll introduce the basics of the Emarsys loyalty solution. With a Emarsys loyalty, you can drive customer engagement and lifetime value with a personalized loyalty program. It allows you to assign rewards and special statuses to your customers based on their interactions, level of engagement, or based on their spending. You can control how and what they get in return. In your A Emarsys account, you can set up and manage a loyalty plan. Use your loyalty plan to control loyalty tiers, benefits, actions, vouchers, and much more. Choose your loyalty plan based on your business model to realize and communicate your strategy to your customers. I hope that you enjoyed this session and thank you for your attention.

### 8

Increasing Customer Lifetime Value with Loyalty

### 9

Emarsys Loyalty programs enhance customer lifetime value without reducing profits. The approach is not limited to offering discounts but uses point or spent-based systems to provide exclusive rewards, such as early access to special deals.​

### 10

Unique benefits are also provided:​

### 11

Established SAP Emarsys platform connection​

### 12

​Uniform and consistent customer experiences​

### 13

​Immediate responses based on customer behavior
