Tiers and Benefits | It utilizes tiers to assign various benefits customers can acquire. Rewards can be personalized, and members can transition between tiers based on specific actions or purchases.

### 28（表格/行）

Rewards and Redemption Management | This involves rewards like vouchers and exclusive access. Vouchers can be earned through points or actions or given out freely. Exclusive access allows members to access restricted parts of a website, such as special promotions.

### 29（表格/行）

Actions | Emarsys Loyalty Actions drive customer engagement through individualized interactions. They permit businesses to create specific messages, incentives, and rules for each customer's situation, such as rewarding members for purchases or brand recommendations.

### 30（表格/行）

Member Management | Emarsys Loyalty users can track loyalty statuses and activities, establish and manage groups, and carry out manual tasks like adding and downloading points.​

### 31（表格/行）

Loyalty Dashboard | Emarsys Loyalty enables users to track the efficiency and status of their loyalty programs via the Loyalty Dashboard.​

### 32（表格/行）

Loyalty Points | Status points determine the member's tier, while spend points are system currency for payments, voucher redemptions, and exclusive rewards.​

### 33（表格/行）

Loyalty Wallet | This is a ready-made embedded solution for e-commerce websites that requires minimal IT skills to configure. It lets website visitors join and log into Loyalty, displays their balances and activities, and earn and redeem points for gifts and rewards.

### 3

Building Web Channel Campaigns

### 4

Web Channel Overview

### 5

SAP Emarsys Web Channel is a feature that helps you deliver customized marketing content on your website. It uses real-time and historical data from your SAP Emarsys database to target specific customer segments or unidentified visitors. Thus, you can convert anonymous visitors into customers.​

### 6

Let’s check out this video to learn more about Web Channel.​

### 7

Welcome In this video, you'll learn about web channel. This channel allows you to display personalized content on your website using all the customer information available in your SAP Emarsys database, including real time session data and historical CRM data. Web channel offers different types of web campaigns. The embedded campaign replaces a selected element on your website with your personalized message. The overlay campaign appears above your normal website content so you can drive retention or turn your anonymous visitors into loyal customers. And if you want to be more discreet with the browsing experience, use a ribbon campaign. It's displayed at the top or bottom of the page and can inform your visitor of updates, such as changes to your terms and conditions, delivery times, or on. I hope that you enjoyed this session and thank you for your attention.

### 8

Web Channel Campaign Types

### 9

Here are examples and use cases of the Embedded, Overlay, and Ribbon campaign types.​

### 10

### 14（表格/行）

### 16（表格/行）

### 17（表格/行）

### 18（表格/行）

Web Channel/Campaigns tab/Session Filters step | Session filtering based on site language, URL, cart or purchase value, device, login status, or 'stops targeting after click.’

### 19（表格/行）

Web Channel/Campaigns tab/Trigger and Scheduling step | Various display frequency and trigger options, like overlay appearance upon opening or leaving the website.

Content Blocks Overview​

### 22

### 23

发言人 01:07

### 24

Welcome In this video, you'll learn about web channel similar to block based on emails, web campaigns are based on content blocks. Before creating a new web campaign, let's check how to manage them. In Emarsys provides pre-built blocks that you can copy and then customize. You can also create new ones from scratch or contact a Emarsys to request tailor-made brand specific blocks from the professional services team. Once you have a block, you can edit, copy, preview, archive, or delete it. Use the search block type and status filters to find what you need. ### 3

Optimizing Mobile First Marketing

### 4

Mobile First Approach Overview

### 5

In a mobile first approach, creators design digital content primarily for mobile devices, such as smartphones, and then adapt it for larger screens.

### 6

With more people using smartphones for browsing and shopping, search engines prioritize mobile-friendly content and sites.

### 7

This approach aligns with enhancing mobile apps and ensuring a seamless user experience, helping companies increase user interaction and boost sales or conversions.

### 8

Six Reasons to Use Mobile First Marketing

### 9

Smartphones are now the primary tool for online shopping and browsing. Retailers can track customer location, aiding marketers with consumer behavior insights. This technology leads to personalized shopping experiences and higher customer engagement.

### 10

Consumers interact with brands by multiple mobile platforms, such as Instagram, Twitter, and Facebook. A well-designed mobile site accessible from these third-party providers can boost traffic and enhance mobile conversions.

### 11

Successful mobile first strategies often involve creating a dedicated mobile brand app or marketplace. These strategies enable more excellent consumer personalization features, such as saving your credit card or recording your shopping history, and offer an extra layer of security and privacy.

### 12

Mobile apps help re-engage customers via targeted messages. In-app messaging aids in feedback and updates, boosting app retention. Event-triggered messages have higher click-through and conversion rates than scheduled ones.

### 13

Push notifications help provide personalized offers, leading to a higher return rate and a boost in In-app engagement. They help increase promotional revenue fivefold.

### 14

In a mobile first strategy, SMS is a key tool to provide direct and efficient communication with users regardless of internet availability. SMS marketing is less intrusive and has the highest instant open and read rates.

### 15

Mobile First Details

### 16

SAP Emarsys supports businesses’ mobile first strategy through its services and user-friendly products, such as Mobile Engage In-app messaging and SMS integration.​

### 17

Businesses can use these tools to improve customer communication and personalize their experience. ​

### 18

SAP Emarsys provides user-friendly solutions that are suitable for a mobile-focused strategy. ​

### 19

Aligning Mobile First Approach with Omnichannel Marketing

### 20

Embracing a mobile first approach is crucial to implement a successful omnichannel marketing strategy because most users access content and shop through mobile devices. This ensures a consistent, seamless, and engaging customer experience across all platforms.​

### 21

Here is a summary of some actionable steps:

### 22

Optimize websites and emails for mobile viewing.​

### 23

Adopt a responsive or mobile first web design.​

### 24

Enhance or build mobile apps for improved customer interaction.​

### 25

Include mobile payment options for seamless transactions.​

### 26

Utilize SMS marketing for effective direct communications.​

### 27

Implement Push notifications and In-app messages in your apps​

### 28

Leverage location-based services for targeted marketing.​

### 29

Use mobile user data for a personalized customer experience.​

### 30

Ensure uniformity in content and design across all channels.

### 31

Regularly track and analyze mobile metrics to refine strategy.​

### 32

Leverage social media platforms catering to mobile users.​

### 33

Integrate mobile customer support for instant assistance.

### 34

Best Practices for Effective Mobile Email Marketing

### 35

With the rise in mobile use for online activities, the chances of your emails being viewed on a desktop are decreasing. To maximize your email marketing campaign value and meet customer needs, adopt a mobile first approach.

### 36

Let’s explore some key practices for creating mobile-friendly emails that increase conversions and promote repeat purchases.

### 37

Keep your Content Concise
Consider subscribers' limited time by condensing long emails with too many images. Adopt a minimalist approach emphasizing brief yet valuable content that highlights benefits to the recipient.

### 38

Opt for a Clean Layout
Evoke emotions with email content without cluttered designs. Since many people view emails on mobile, ensure simplicity with a one-column layout and well-sized, properly-spaced buttons for effortless navigation and interaction.

### 39

Choose Impactful Images
In mobile first emails, prioritize concise text overlays and high-quality, quick-loading images. Personalize images with advanced platforms based on subscriber behavior and preferences to boost engagement.

### 40

Use Cross-Links
Deep links, appearing as normal hyperlinks, direct subscribers within your app when clicked instead of opening a webpage. Their use in campaigns and Automation provides a seamless customer experience, boosting In-app conversions.

### 41

Leverage Pre-Header Text
Though sometimes neglected, pre-headers are essential for email engagement. They help define the content and accentuate key messages and calls to action, stimulating clicks and enhancing engagement, ultimately increasing open rates.

### 42

Ensure Clear and Noticeable Call to Action (CTA)
Ensure your call to action (CTA) is clear and singular to avoid confusion. Highlight your value proposition in your email and place the CTA prominently to facilitate subscriber progression based on their interest in your offer.

### 43

Test and Preview your Emails
To avoid credibility-damaging errors, thoroughly test your marketing emails. Utilize your email marketing platform's features to preview emails across different devices and email providers, and send test emails to check formatting, correct rendering, and proper merge tag updates.

### 44

Incorporating Mobile Design and App-Focused Marketing

### 45

Products and Features for the Mobile First Approach

### 46

SAP Emarsys supports the mobile first strategy with several functionalities.​

### 47

Mobile Engage enables you to connect with your customers through your mobile application:​

### 48

Push messages are notifications sent directly to a user's mobile device.​

### 49

In-app messages are content users receive while actively using a mobile application.​

### 50

Inbox campaigns are messages sent to the mobile app, permitting customers to read them at their preferred time.​

### 51

SMS/MMS enables you to create, automate, and send text messages, connecting you with more of your customers, even when they're offline.​

### 52

Mobile Wallet lets you create and send mobile wallet passes for your customers, giving them easy access to loyalty cards and coupons on their smartphones.​

### 53

Mobile first support by Block-Based Email:​

### 54

The mobile-optimized prebuilt email template includes responsive design, a Mobile Wallet block, and customization advice.

### 55

Mobile first email preview and editor to adjust subject lines, preheader text, and content for a more efficient and effective mobile design.

### 56

Single Edit View: Lock Screen

### 57

Dual Edit View: Lock Screen and Message List

### 58

Dual Edit View: Mobile Email and Message List

### 59

Let's check out some of the Mobile Engage key features.

### 60

1. Mobile Engage: Geofencing

### 61

Integrate location data to automatically send alerts, updates, or deals to customers entering or exiting a specific location.

### 62

2. Mobile Engage: Personalization

### 63

Personalize messages with user data to grow your customer base, increase data collection, and boost sales.

### 64

3. Mobile Engage: Automation and Cross-Channel Journeys

### 65

Enhance user experience with immediate and automated action-based Push, In-app, and Inbox messages. You can integrate various channels and enhance cross-channel user experiences.

### 66

4. Mobile Engage: Dashboard and Reporting

### 67

Use the dashboard or campaign-level reporting to track results and revenue effects, pinpointing growth prospects and areas for improvement.

### 68

Mobile-Related Channels

### 69

SAP Emarsys Automation provides the leverage to create Automation programs driven by mobile event triggers, enhancing your real-time customer interaction. It offers the flexibility to incorporate all supported channels, build multi-step customer journeys, and use mobile-optimized content for effective engagement. You can include channel-specific and mobile-specific segments, such as SMS or Mobile Engage, leveraging mobile app and behavioral data for improved targeting and personalization in your marketing initiatives.

### 80

### 81

### 84

​In this scenario, we aim to greet your new app users, create great first impressions, and convert them into loyal customers. We use various channels and features to lead these individuals and encourage their purchasing actions.

### 85

Mobile Engage: In-app, Push, Inbox

Mobile Wallet

### 3

Creating Mobile Wallet Campaigns

### 4

Mobile Wallet Overview

### 5

The Mobile Wallet feature in SAP Emarsys enables users to securely store and use digital passes, such as loyalty cards, coupons, and vouchers in Apple Wallet and Google Wallet. It supports use cases such as loyalty schemes and voucher discounts, enabling users to dynamically update and access their digital passes directly from their mobile devices.​

### 6

You can create, test, and evaluate campaigns in the Mobile Wallet channel. Use Email, In-app messages, or Web Channel campaigns to deliver personalized digital passes such as coupons, loyalty cards, and vouchers, enabling users to store and access them in Apple Wallet or Google Wallet.​

### 7

Let's check out the video to learn more about Mobile Wallet.

### 8

Welcome In this video, we'll introduce our mobile wallet feature that helps you create mobile pass campaigns. Apple and Google Wallets allow users to store and use electronic bank cards, id's, coupons, and other digital passes, also known as mobile or wallet passes. This function makes your customers lives easier and helps them engage offline with your brand.

### 9

Before creating SAP Emarsys mobile wallet campaigns, you have to go through a one time setup process when you set unique, identifiable, and upload the necessary certificates. Let's start with the wallet configuration. Navigate to Channels Mobile Wallet Wallet configuration tab and click on Add Configuration. Here I add an internal configuration name and upload the Apple certificate. Once uploaded, I fill the mandatory fields, then I upload the Google certificate and add my issuer ID. Our documentation helps you how to create and set these files. I save my configuration in the overview.

### 10

You can edit or archive configurations. Please note that archived configurations can not be edited and configurations and use can not be archived until the related wallet campaigns have also been archived, thanks for watching.

### 11

Key Terminology for Mobile Wallet Setup

### 12

Let’s check the key words you need to know for creating and using a Mobile Wallet campaign.​

### 13（表格/行）

Terminology | Description

### 14（表格/行）

Wallet configuration | Apple and Google prerequisites for a Wallet campaign creation

### 15（表格/行）

Wallet campaign | The campaign created in the Mobile Wallet channel​

### 16（表格/行）

Wallet Pass | The end user generates the loyalty card, voucher, or coupon

### 17（表格/行）

Parent campaign | The campaign that distributes the Wallet Campaign. Current parent channels - Email, In-app, and Web Channel

### 18（表格/行）

Pass update | Re-generation of the Wallet pass either by a mass update (broadcast) or an individual update (AC initiated)​
​
The Wallet passes that users have already downloaded onto their devices and are getting updated

### 19

Understanding How Mobile Wallet Works

### 20

You can create, test, and launch your Mobile Wallet campaign.​

### 21

You can share the Wallet campaign with users through parent channels such as Email, In-app, and Web Channel.​

### 22

Upon receiving a URL, the user clicks on the Wallet link, 'Generates a pass.'​

### 23

A personalized pass is created within the Wallet service, adhering to the design of the Wallet campaign, and sent to users through Google or Apple APIs.​

### 24

Users can download the pass into their Wallet app, making it accessible offline.​

### 25

You can update the Wallet campaign either broadcast-style or individually; the corresponding Wallet passes get updated.

### 26

Mobile Wallet Campaign Tab

### 27

The Wallet Campaign tab contains all the details about your Mobile Wallet campaigns, such as:

### 28

Campaign Name​

### 29

Template Type​

### 30

Status ​

### 31

Created​

### 32

You can also:

### 33

View the detailed reporting page for the Wallet Campaign.​

### 34

Change the design and custom fields.​

### 35

Archive a Wallet Campaign.​

### 36

You can add campaigns once you configure your Apple and Google Wallet in the configuration tab.

### 37

Creating a Mobile Wallet Campaign

### 38

Let’s create our Mobile Wallet Campaign.​

### 39

Choose the type of pass you prefer to create.

### 40

Coupon: Valid for one use only ​

### 41

Loyalty: To have relevant Loyalty program information on the pass ​

### 42

Voucher: Show a reduced price or designated value

### 43

1. Select the Coupon type and create the content.

### 44

Campaign Settings:

### 45

• Add an internal name for your campaign and choose a ‘Wallet configuration’ you set previously.

### 46

• Enter a ‘Description’ and ‘Provider Name’. These appear on the back of the pass.

### 47

• Choose a 'Code Type' or select 'Show No Code' (for example, ‘QR code’).

### 48

• Define the 'Code Value' or add a personalized field to display a unique code from the voucher pool.

### 49

• Add low- and high-resolution images of the icon from the Media Database. These images are used in lock screen notifications.

### 50

• If you wish, specify the ‘Expiry Date’ (for example, end of the month).

### 51

Style Settings:

### 52

• Change the background color.

### 53

• For Apple devices, adjust the color of the title and body.

### 54

In the Preview/Live Editor:

### 55

Insert your logo, a message, and an image about the incentive in the live preview.

### 56

Switch to the back view to add provider information. Here, you can drag and drop tokens from the Personalization tab.​

### 57

Use the device preview to review your campaign's appearance on iOS and Android platforms.

### 58

Click the 'Test Pass' button to generate a Wallet pass and test the Wallet campaign on both platforms.​

### 59

Once the test meets your requirements, click the 'Finish' button to set the Wallet campaign's status to 'Ready to Launch.’

### 60

After launching the Wallet campaign, it can be integrated into a primary campaign using a related Personalization token. ​

### 61

Possible parent campaigns include Email, In-app, and Web Channel. ​

### 62

Click on the 'Create Draft' button to make bulk updates to the launched Mobile Wallet campaign.​

### 63

In the versioning drop-down, you can 'Save as Draft', or save and relaunch it.

### 64

Mobile Wallet Reporting Page

### 65

After you send out your Wallet campaign, you can track its performance on the Reporting screen. ​

### 66

You can filter by platform to view metrics only iOS or Android users perform.​

### 67

Generated: The recipient clicks the unique Mobile Pass URL, such as 'Add to Google Wallet' or 'Add to Apple Wallet', and previews the pass.

### 68

Downloaded: The user saves the Mobile Pass to their Mobile Wallet by clicking 'Add' after previewing.​

### 69

Updated: This is the number of times a saved wallet pass has been changed, regardless of mass or individual changes.​

### 70

Removed: The user deliberately deletes the individual Mobile Pass from their Mobile Wallet.

### 3

Creating SMS

### 4

SMS Overview​

### 5

SAP Emarsys SMS helps you create, automate, and deliver personalized text messages from your SAP Emarsys account to customers' mobile devices based on their interactions with your brand. ​

### 6

With this channel, you can reach a global customer base even when they are offline.​

### 7

In selected regions and with vendors, SAP Emarsys also supports creating and managing MMS campaigns, enabling a richer communication with enhanced content such as images.

### 8

Let’s check out the video to learn more about SAP Emarsys SMS.​

### 9

Hello, in this video we will introduce the Sap MS SMS and Mms channel. With this channel, you can create, automate, and deliver messages from your account to reach more of your customer base, even when they're offline.

### 10

发言人 00:18

_重复来源：new20+21-mobile engagement management_原文.docx_

### 11

First, you need an SMS vendor that provides SMS messaging services. All SMS vendors that have integrated with a Emarsys are listed in the list of providers. Should the desired SMS vendor not be listed, I Emarsys offers a partner API to allow SMS vendors to integrate with SAP Emarsys customer engagement. Once your provider is set up under management SMS settings, you can add countries or regions and define additional settings like inbound numbers, fee per SMS and test message number.

### 12

发言人 00:56

### 13

You can set customized Url's which will be used instead of the default in Emarsys domains. When we shorten your links, this aids with brand recognition and user engagement. Just click here, enter the domain name, and select the type. Once you add your domain Emarsys checks if it can be reached via our redirect logic. When the domain status is validated, you can use it in your SMS campaigns. Your contacts need to opt in to receive marketing Smss or mmss, however, for transactional messages such as system notifications or password or retrieval, this is not required. Before using SMS, make sure you comply with the local regulations of your target countries. I hope you enjoyed this session, thanks for your attention.

### 14

SAP Emarsys has different types of SMS categories:

### 15

Transactional SMS Messages: No opt-in is required; they contain static information such as system notifications, password retrievals, essential updates, ignoring opt-out features, and shipping and delivery updates. Set up once; it works automatically. ​

### 16

Commercial/Marketing SMS Messages: Must be opt-in, easy opt-out. They are used for offers, deals, helpful tips, and information. An option to create unique short links is available. ​

### 17

After creating messages in the SAP Emarsys SMS editor, you can initiate or incorporate batch SMS campaigns into established or new Automation programs. These campaigns can be sent through either SAP Emarsys' provider or the customer's designated provider.

### 18

SMS Functionalities​

### 19

Recurring and on-event transactional SMS messages through Automation programs ​​

### 20

Ad hoc batch SMS campaigns with a simple interface​

### 21

Unique short link unsubscribe URLs guiding to customized landing pages

### 22

Smart mobile filtering to filter opted-out or invalid numbers​​

### 23

Campaign cost estimator​

### 24

Deduplication of mobile phone numbers​​

### 25

Full campaign reporting ​​

### 26

Dynamic sender ID to send the SMS from a Short Code must be done using the From Name​​

### 27

Alphanumeric sender name, for example, your brand name

### 28

Numeric sender ID, for example, your Customer Support helpline

### 29

Unsubscribing using the STOP keyword as well as inbound message keywords​

### 30

SMS segmentation​

### 31

SMS Benefits

### 32

1 Smart Mobile Filtering in SAP Emarsys SMS reduces costs by preventing campaigns from being sent to invalid numbers, hard bounces, and opted-out contacts.

### 33

2 Contacts can opt-out through 2-way messaging (replying with the ‘STOP’ keyword) or clicking an unsubscribe URL.

### 34

3 2-way messaging enables customers to trigger Automation programs.

### 35

4 SAP Emarsys SMS can be combined with other channels for marketing programs to get advanced reporting on one platform.

### 36

5 SAP Emarsys SMS ensures a unified personalization experience across channels.

Managing Mobile Engage Campaigns

### 4

Mobile Engage Overview

### 5

SAP Emarsys Mobile Engage enables businesses to integrate their mobile applications with SAP Emarsys in order to send real time messages.​

### 6

Mobile Engage connects through Push notifications, In-app and Inbox messages, sending personalized content to improve engagement, retention, and revenue.

### 7

Mobile Engage Key Features

### 8

1 Connect contacts’ email and mobile data

### 9

2 Track mobile browsing and e-commerce activity

### 10

3 Coordinate cross-channel Automation

### 11

4 Send Push, In-app, or Inbox messages according to your use cases

### 12

5 Manage marketing and transactional campaigns

### 13

6 Personalize your Push, In-app, and Inbox campaigns to specific audiences

### 14

7 Utilize Silent Push and Geofencing

### 15

8 Full integration with the Automation and Tactics

### 16

9 Customer-centric personalization

### 17

10 Product recommendations

### 18

11 Actionable intelligence for In-app experiences

### 19

12 Messages are delivered to the device

### 20

13 In-app notifications

### 21

14 Rich media

### 22

Mobile Engage Push Overview

### 23

SAP Emarsys Mobile Engage Push is a feature that sends personalized Push notification messages to mobile app users, enabling direct and targeted engagement with the audience.​

### 24

Let’s check out the video to learn more about Mobile Engage Push.

### 25

In this video, we're going to introduce mobile engage push notifications. Mobile apps are becoming the most important channel to communicate with your customers. Driving engagement with mobile engagement is very effective as you can send push messages to your mobile application subscribers across multiple platforms and devices.

### 26

Push notifications are messages that pop up on smartphones. They look similar to text messages or mobile alerts, but can only reach subscribers who have installed your app and give explicit permission to get these notifications. The users don't have to be on the app or using their devices to receive them. When a user installs your app and gives permission to accept push messages, a push token is generated. The Push token is used to deliver the push message to the device. The message you create in the Emarsys platform can be sent through an automation program or directly to the devices of users included in a segment.

Mobile Engage Inbox Overview

### 129

The SAP Emarsys Mobile Engage Inbox feature displays messages in a central area of your mobile app. It enables contacts to receive persistent, opt-in-free notifications that they can read and engage with repeatedly.​

### 130

Let’s check out the video to learn more about Mobile Engage Inbox campaigns.

### 131

Welcome In this video, we will present you mobile engage inbox campaigns. Why inbox? A mobile app inbox is very similar to an email inbox, so your messages will be displayed in the central area of your mobile. One key differentiator is that contacts don't need to opt in to receive notifications to receive inbox messages. Also, your contacts will be engaged with inbox messages longer and will read them through repeatedly. Unlike push notifications, inbox messages are persistent. They don't disappear like push. Thank you for your attention.

### 3

Exploring Program Reporting

### 4

Program Reporting Overview

### 5

Program Reporting enables you to monitor how well your Automation programs guide incoming contacts and provide valuable insights, including:​

### 6

The program's processed traffic volume ​

### 7

The number of messages triggered by the program split into channels​

### 8

The revenue ​generated by the program​

### 9

Details at program and node levels​

### 10

Program Reporting also offers a comprehensive overview of your program's performance: ​

### 11

Program history with key metrics ​

### 12

Program revenue generation with revenue-related statistics ​

### 13

Program details with in-depth numbers at program and node levels​

### 14

These statistics can assist in analyzing and troubleshooting your program if any issues arise.

### 15

For a detailed analysis of a specific marketing campaign, we recommend accessing the reporting screens for each channel or using Smart Insight.

### 16

Program Reporting Examples

### 17

Here are a few examples when the Program Reporting proves helpful:​

### 18

Operational monitoring: Use the Program Reporting to confirm that new programs are functioning as expected in their initial weeks. ​

### 19

Program performance monitoring: Use the Program Reporting page to regularly monitor contact and message-related metrics, such as Entered, Messages Triggered, Finished, and Exited. ​

### 20

Troubleshooting: Leverage the reporting on a contact-level in Interactions programs to address customer complaints about missed or diverted emails.

### 21

Checking the Program Reporting Page

### 22

To access the Program Reporting, navigate to Automation ﹥ Automation Programs and click the Reporting icon next to a program.

### 23

You can adjust the results at the top of the page in the General Settings.​

### 24

By default, the results are displayed for the last seven days. To modify this timeframe, click the datetime picker.​

### 25

Switch between Minutely, Hourly, Daily, Weekly, and Monthly views.

### 26

Display contact-level results by entering their email address.​

### 27

Modify your program by clicking Edit Program at the top right corner.

### 28

The 'History' widget showcases the essential metrics associated with your program.​

### 29

Entered: This is the number of times a contact entered the program. ​

### 30

Messages Triggered: This is the total number of messages triggered by the program across all channels, such as email, SMS, Push, In-app, and Web Push messages.​

### 31

Finished: This is the number of times a contact reached a finish node, irrespective of message receipt or response. ​

### 32

Exited: This is the number of times a contact exited the program without reaching a Finish node. The exit from the program can happen due to the following:​

### 33

Nodes filter out contacts, such as Decision, Segment, Exclude, Quick filter, or Email behavior check nodes. ​

### 34

Deletion of nodes​

### 35

Change in program status to Frozen or Fail-safe ​

### 36

Expiration of the trigger event (trigger event timeout)​

### 37

Contacts are leaving the program upon meeting exit criteria.​

### 38

Contacts exiting the program due to the participation settings.​

### 39

Hover over the "Exited" metric to view details.

### 40

The 'Revenue Generated by the Program' widget displays the revenue generated by the program.​

### 41

Attributed Revenue: Total revenue attributed to this program​

### 42

Number of Purchases: Total purchases made by contacts participating in this program​

### 43

Average Order Value: Calculated by dividing the Attributed Revenue by the Number of Purchases​

### 44

You can switch to the Revenue Analytics page by clicking Revenue Analytics.

### 45

The 'Program Details' widget displays your program and node-level metrics.

### 46

Numbers above nodes indicate the number of contacts that pass through them.​

### 47

Colored bars above the numbers indicate the following: ​

### 48

Dark blue: Contacts that arrived at this node and continued to the next one​

### 49

Red: Contacts that exited the program at this node​

### 50

Light blue: Contacts currently waiting at this node​

### 51

Green: Contacts that continued on the ‘Yes path’ of the node​

### 52

Orange: Contacts that continued on the ‘No path' of the node​

### 53

Other: This shows various metrics, such as contacts who skipped the node because it was disabled or the program-triggering event expired.​

### 54

At the bottom of the widget, you can view the program's settings: ​

### 55

Schedule: The program's schedule ​

### 56

Participation: The program's participation settings​

### 57

Exit criteria: The program's exit criteria

### 58

When you click a node, it displays detailed information and percentage-based metrics on the right side of the widget.​

### 59

Here, you can do the following: ​

### 60

Open the node dialog by clicking the icon.​

### 61

Access the Email Analysis page by clicking the "Reporting" icon. ​

### 62

Edit the email campaign by clicking the "Edit" icon.​

### 63

Review various node-level statistics, including the number of contacts who Arrived, Continued, are Waiting, or Exited at the specific node.

### 64

Tracking the Impact of Omnichannel Marketing Campaigns with Analytics

### 65

Campaign Analytics Overview

### 66

Campaign Analytics is a tool for cross-channel trend reporting. It enables the performance evaluation of campaigns across different channels over time for specific segments. ​

### 67

Dimensions for analyzing omnichannel campaigns include: ​

### 68

Channels: Comparing campaign results based on different channels ​

### 69

Segments: Filtering campaign results by segments​

### 70

Metrics: Generating trend reports for each channel

### 71

With Campaign Analytics, you can:

### 72

1 Regularly monitor campaign performance to address any immediate concerns.

### 73

2 Evaluate marketing performance across various dimensions such as channel, segment, and time.

### 74

3 Compare campaigns to understand which performed better for specific contact groups.

### 75

4 Assess the revenue impact of each channel to determine if it meets its goals.

### 76

Checking Campaign Analytics

### 77

Select the date range.​

### 78

Optionally filter data by the segment on the Campaign Analytics page.​

### 79

Click "Apply" after choosing the search parameters.

### 80

The report loads with three main views: ​

### 81

Engagement (Trends)​

### 82

Engagement Summary​

### 83

Performance​

### 84

The report shows each view per channel, where you can choose a channel for analysis.​

### 85

Download the widget data in CSV by clicking "Export Report" in the top right corner.​

### 86

Switch channels using the Select Channel selector to view metrics for Email, Mobile In-app, Mobile Inbox, Mobile Push, and SMS channels for the specified period.​

### 87

Select multiple metrics simultaneously (available metrics depend on the selected channel).​

### 88

Toggle views between total interactions (events) and rates, such as open rate and click rate.​

### 89

You can click "Explore on Campaign Level" to see engagement widgets for each channel in a campaign-level view.

### 90

The Engagement Summary provides metrics for each channel across the entire period, similar to those in the Engagement view. These show totals for the period rather than a trend line. ​

### 91

The Performance metrics are based on the respective channels' attributed revenue and purchase rate. The metrics are consistent across channels for easy comparison.

### 92

Metrics depend on the chosen channel.​

### 93

The Performance widget appears when the Revenue Attribution feature is on, and purchase data is available in SAP Emarsys.

### 94

Click "Explore on Campaign Level" to view all channel metrics for each selected campaign. You can compare the performance of the selected campaigns on the channel ​widgets.​

### 95

Filter the results for specific campaigns by clicking on the selected campaigns to open a selection dialog.​

### 96

After selecting specific campaigns to filter results, you can return to the "All Campaigns" selection by refreshing the page or clicking the Apply button. ​

### 97

You can change the filters at the top of the page, such as date range or segment, and reset the campaign selection.

### 98

Let’s check out the video to learn more about how to use Campaign Analytics.

### 99

Hi there, in this short video, I show you how to use the campaign analytics page to view the performance of our new omnichannel program for greeting and converting new app users.

### 100

Let's review the campaign's performances. First, I select the date range under the Engagement widget. I click this button to access campaign level reporting. Here you see an engagement widget for each channel, which allows you to filter the results for specific campaigns and compare them. I choose the emails from the drop down menu and select the clicked clicked on mobile and purchased metrics to measure their success. Moving on to mobile in app, I select the campaign used in the program, followed by the clicked and purchased data. Next up is the mobile inbox. Like before, I select the campaign and the relevant metrics coming to Mobile Push, I repeat the process. Here, I need the opened and purchased data. Lastly, there is the SMS.

### 101

After selecting my campaign, I choose clicked and purchased. By hovering over the charts, you can observe daily figures and draw conclusions such as the number of email clicks made on mobile phones seems relatively low. There is a dip in mobile performance on a date. While the email campaign demonstrated high rates. I can export these metrics to make further analysis. I hope this session was helpful, thanks for watching.
